### SQLite compiler work measurement (receipt 109)

The [Rust harness](../../../tests/support/sqlite_copying.rs) installs the exact
provider and SQLite manifests and binding, then calls
`run_path_with_foreign_runtime` on an Ash entry path. Only the entry and its
loaded import dependencies enter Ash compilation. Copying all fixture files into
the temporary directory does not compile unrelated entries. Embedded strings
such as the reset program in the [copying test](../../../tests/provider_sqlite_copying.rs)
are written as Ash files and compiled through the same public driver. Rust
fixture setup, assertions, Cargo compilation and native SQLite execution do not
consume the compiler's `TypeWorkBudget`.

This budget counts explicit compiler work charges, including structural visits,
copies and retained analysis records. It does not measure elapsed time or source
length. The compiler has separate ledgers for several stages; there is no single
account for every compiler phase or the complete Rust test. Imported generic
provider definitions and generated evidence contribute to compiler work, but
their presence alone does not establish that the implementation cost is
necessary.

Common's profile 109 runs the actual copying, session and control tests against
its coherent prepared fourteen-operation contract:

```sh
CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=1 cargo test --locked -p ash --test provider_sqlite_copying --test provider_sqlite_sessions --test provider_sqlite_control --no-fail-fast -- --nocapture --test-threads=1
```

Temporary compiler instrumentation records charge sites and analysis components
without changing admitted charges or limits. The command exits 101 in 118
seconds, and all four required larger programs still return
`Typing(Internal(TypeResourceExhausted))` before native execution. The original
productive source bytes are restored after the diagnostic run. Its source pins,
runner, raw log and result are retained in
`~/.codex/ash-provider-evidence/common-resource-support-2026-10-04/provider-budget-cold-profile-109/`.

| Program | Used units | Refused units | Exact profiled compiler charge |
| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |
| Full Unicode/BLOB/null/empty copying | 327679 | 2 | `core/capture/control.rs:450`, copying an analysis slice. |
| Bound-text NUL refusal with completion reports | 327679 | 2 | The same analysis-slice copying charge. |
| Reset, preserved binding and clear-to-null | 327680 | 1 | `core/capture/value.rs:3414`, the next pending slot in `Analysis::close`. |
| Generic row collection and file persistence | 327680 | 1 | `core/capture.rs:988`, the next Core node in `free_values`. |

These are the first refused charges, not the complete work required to compile
each program. `Analysis::close` completes capture/dependency propagation; it is
not native connection close. No single remaining defective algorithm is
established by these locations.

The bounded trace does not distinguish finite analysis work from a progression
that would never converge. Excessive repeated work is one hypothesis, not an
established cause. Diagnosis must also examine whether context, slot, edge and
fact creation have a finite bound, and whether queue processing reaches a fixed
point. Growth samples can reveal a suspected progression, but a truncated run
alone cannot prove termination or nontermination.

The reviewed cold evidence-transfer measurements attribute 14,821 of 19,711
units to capture specialization in the copying/NUL prefix, and 11,923 of 17,053
in the reset prefix. Those are components of sampled cold transfers, not the
whole compiler budget or potential savings. Each actual evidence slot has only
one cold closure transfer in the sampled prefixes, so reuse across repeated
cold transfers does not explain these failures. The independent interpretation
is retained in common's `provider-evidence-cold-runtime-review-109/review.md`.
Common continues shared compiler diagnosis; SQLite keeps all required programs
and assertions, imports only verified support, and makes no limit increase.

`src/core/capture.rs`, `src/elaborate.rs` and their carrier inventory in
`tests/architecture.rs`. It preserves the original function
roots, exact creation and invocation contexts, full owner Type/Row arguments,
late dependencies and independent Core checks. It changes no resource semantics,
foreign ABI, production work ceiling or depth limit.

Queued evaluation now returns a transient setup outcome from the actual matched
expression. Only successful fixed setup can publish `EdgesInstalled`, after its
complete ordered edge propagation and paid publication. Dynamic expressions and
unfinished inputs remain live. Every visit still propagates its ordered edges;
the completed setup phase is neither a cached answer nor capture permission.

The F2 structural product shares lexical and abstract-capture child enumeration
while keeping separate concern bits, cuts and discovery order. Lexical output
still uses the complete binding filter; method replacement can cut lexical
traversal while abstract evidence continues. A temporary scalar recipe defers
abstract symbol/evidence allocation until after lexical and unchanged metadata
installation. This preserves original graph coordinates and malformed-input
phase ordering. The tradeoff is a fallibly allocated recipe and concern state
retained through one producer call. No graph/body cache is added; peak memory
bytes were not measured, so this is not a measured peak-memory reduction.

Final Effect global synthesis borrows one completed checked source scheme and
its non-freshened lowering. Original source, requirement, kinded-parameter and
lowered-type comparisons precede the private consuming binder loan. It omits a
temporary projection and `Forall` construction that the old final branch then
discarded, pays its three-unit selection cost, and still clones the actual
lowered Core scheme into its iterative owner. Other elaboration purposes retain
their original path. The loan grants no independent Core authority.

The measurements reuse authenticated preceding runs rather than repeating the
baseline: receipt 279 has four baseline and four candidate executions; F2 and
FinalBinder each add four candidate executions. Every original public/native
assertion passes under identical explicitly temporary diagnostic completion
ceilings. Normalized fixture inputs match; raw PID contents can differ at the
fixed padded width. Each comparison retains six separate raw owner endpoints
and ordered Binder facts. No owner-budget sum or capacity discount is used.

| F2 product | −1,997 / −5,259 | −5,051 / −7,309 | −1,298 / −1,786 |
| FinalBinder | 0 / 0 | +51 / 0 | 0 / 0 |

### Shared staged borrowed-result admission — 2026-10-07

The common executable binding now distinguishes Immediate from Prepared replies.
Existing adapters call their original immediate method exactly once. A prepared
reply retains unpublished scratch through codomain checking and complete
decoding/work/storage admission, then consumes one commit against the same
authenticated operation, defining module and live ordered owner views. No native
owner or keepalive transfers to the pending object. The API documentation and
[experimental
semantics](semantics.md#deferred-publication-of-borrowed-native-results)
distinguish the consuming Rust ownership property from trusted adapter
publication/drop behavior and host allocation assumptions. This adds no source
syntax or typed arena allocation.

The first behavioral baseline observed native publication before a malformed
result was rejected, while the valid control passed. The staged implementation
passes six boundary cases across a checked Ash fixture and the direct host API:
malformed result, valid commit, unchanged legacy immediate behavior,
dropped/stale/foreign pending commits, exact final decode-work refusal, and the
existing acquisition publication refusal. The work cutoff authenticates the
unique original operation and node adjacency and checks prefills zero and seven;
refusal leaves the pending commit abandoned, preserves the owner for host
teardown and starts no child acquisition.

programs and all 43 test assertions remain unchanged. This changes test
implementation only; it adds no Ash semantics or resource capability.
